#6756. Your Expert-Backed Guide to Preparing for Top Surgery

From handling logistics ahead of time to a shopping list, experts share some of the best ways to make recovery from this major operation a little bit more manageable.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Getting ready to go under the knife can be scary—even when it’s for a procedure you’ve wanted for as long as you can remember. If you’re preparing for top surgery (a gender-affirming chest surgery to remove or augment breast tissue) right now, chances are you’re probably trying to ease your pre-op anxiety by googling every question you can possibly think of. And that’s normal! But it can also lead you down some deep, dark Reddit threads that’ll do nothing but amplify your nerves.

If you’re wondering how to prepare for top surgery without all the panic-inducing anecdotes, you’ve come to the right place. I chatted with three experts and got their best advice on how to get ready for the procedure while preserving your peace of mind.

Before we get into the tips, keep in mind that the most important part of preparing for top surgery is choosing the right surgeon for you. In addition to finding an experienced and gender-affirming doctor, you’ll want to pick someone who can give you the results you’re looking for. “The techniques your surgeon will use can vary quite a lot depending on your breast size and shape, the texture and quality of your skin, and your goals for the procedure,” explains Eric Plemons, PhD, an associate professor of anthropology and cochair of the Transgender Studies Research Cluster at the University of Arizona.

To make sure you’ll be happy with your results, he suggests watching YouTube videos showcasing before-and-afters and looking at post-surgery images online to help you get an idea of what you want. Then be sure to find a surgeon who understands your goals—if they seem like they’re not listening to you, ditch them. Once you’ve made an appointment with a surgeon you trust, here’s everything else the experts recommend doing to prepare for top surgery.

This might seem like a no-brainer, but it’s worth mentioning. Before your surgery, your doctor’s office will normally provide you with instructions on what to do before and after the procedure. They typically include info on dietary guidelines, activity restrictions, wound care, and any medications you might need. Make sure that you read all of the information carefully, and keep it handy for the recovery process. “Following these instructions diligently significantly improves surgical outcomes and ensures a smoother recovery,” explains Scott Mosser, MD, FACS, a plastic surgeon who performs gender confirmation surgery and the founder of The Gender Confirmation Center of San Francisco.

Top surgery is a major surgery, and you’ll likely spend the first one to two weeks at home resting after the procedure. So it’s a good idea to prep your space accordingly. Make sure you’ve got a cozy couch or bed setup ready, and consider getting a mastectomy pillow to make lounging and sleeping more comfortable. The Trans Health Program at Fenway Health also recommends getting other items like a long phone charger, a grabber tool, and a back scratcher to make recovery go a bit smoother.

Also, keep in mind that you’re not supposed to lift your arms above your head in the days following top surgery. To make that easier to navigate, consider moving food and essentials to lower, easy-to-reach spots, suggests Plemons.

The last thing you want to do is spend your recovery period stressing about going back to your job. Be sure you request enough time off to allow yourself to truly rest and heal. Exactly how much time you’ll need to spend OOO varies from person to person, so it’s a good idea to chat it through with your surgeon at one of your pre-op appointments.

One of the main factors that determines how long you’ll need to take off work is how physically demanding your job is. You’ll have restricted movement in the weeks after your surgery, and you won’t be able to lift heavy objects, which could impact your responsibilities, depending on your line of work. “I generally ask patients to identify the most physically strenuous aspect of their job, and together we determine an appropriate leave period,” explains Dr. Mosser.

For your post-op wardrobe, you’ll want a stack of loose-fitting shirts that are easy to put on and take off without raising your arms. (Yup, t-shirts might be off the table for a bit.) Button-up and zip-up options will be your best friends during the recovery period. The loose-fitting aspect is also important because you may need space for surgical drain tubes under your shirt. Tops with interior pockets can also be helpful if you do end up with drains, says Dr. Mosser. And heads-up: Your shirts might get stained during the healing process, so Plemons recommends grabbing some options from the thrift store so that you’re not worried about ruining them.

In order to help your skin adjust post-surgery, you may want to get an emollient-infused moisturizer like Bio-Oil or Palmer’s Cocoa Butter Lotion. As long as it’s okay with your doctor, you can even start applying these one to two weeks before the procedure to start prepping your skin, if you’d like. After the surgery, Dr. Mosser recommends using one of these moisturizers on your chest every morning and evening—but be careful to avoid your incision sites. This will help with skin relaxation and reduce the appearance of stretch marks, if that’s something you’re concerned about, he explains.

Double-check with your surgeon, but after any scabs from the surgery have fallen off, you should also be able to start using silicone scar tape. Dr. Mosser suggests using it for three months to help optimize healing.

If you vape or smoke cigarettes or weed, you’ll need to quit ahead of your top surgery. The American College of Surgeons recommends quitting smoking four to six weeks before any major surgery and staying smoke-free for at least four weeks post-op to lower your risk of complications, and most doctors require a similar timeline for top surgery.

The stakes are high: According to the Gender Confirmation Center of San Francisco, smoking increases the risk of anesthesia complications and postoperative complications like losing a nipple graft, which can require additional surgery to fix. It also delays healing, which can prolong the recovery process and make it more uncomfortable. Dr. Mosser requires all of his patients to stop smoking for at least three weeks before and after top surgery—no exceptions.

You’ll need some help taking care of yourself and your basic needs in the days following top surgery, so talk to your friends and/or family in advance to put together a plan. “We’ve consistently seen better outcomes in patients who have a positive, supportive presence in their lives. The best way to ensure this support is to communicate openly with loved ones in advance, set clear expectations about what assistance might be needed, and cultivate an environment of encouragement and care,” Dr. Mosser says.

Decide who’s going to drive you home after the procedure (you won’t be allowed to drive yourself), and think about who might be a good fit to help you with activities like getting dressed and showering the first few days, suggests Plemons. You’ll probably also need someone to help you with tasks like meal prep, medication reminders, carrying groceries, and caring for any pets for a bit.

In addition to logistical support, you’ll likely need some emotional support during the top surgery process. The procedure is often something you’ve been anticipating for a long time, and it can be an emotional experience. “Such a big event comes with big feelings about your body, about yourself, and about your future,” says Plemons. So think about who you plan to lean on for support as you navigate your feelings both pre- and post-op. Staying connected to your community—whether that be friends, chosen family, or an online community—is really important. “Even small moments of support, like check-ins or affirming conversations, can make a huge difference in feeling cared for during recovery,” adds Jesse Kahn, LCSW-R, CST, director and sex therapist at the Gender & Sexuality Therapy Center in New York.

“Top surgery is a significant emotional and physical milestone, and a range of feelings—excitement, anxiety, or unexpected emotions—are completely normal,” explains Kahn. They recommend giving yourself permission to feel and process your emotions without judgment or criticism. Your emotional support network can help with this, but if you’re already seeing a therapist you may want to schedule a session early on in your recovery. “[A therapy session] can be a good opportunity to just check in, look inward, reflect on what you’re experiencing, and give space for your emotions,” says Kahn. Practicing self-care, managing expectations, and doing activities that bring you comfort are also very important during the top surgery process, they add.

Give yourself something you look forward to by thinking about what you want to wear once you’ve healed. Buy a shirt in a style you’ve always wanted to wear, or plan out an outfit that’ll showcase your post-op body. “Choose something that will fit you well and show off the body you’re excited to have," suggests Plemons. "After soreness and swelling, it is amazing how gratifying it is to slip into that special shirt (or strut around in no shirt at all).”

This article is not intended to be a substitute for medical advice. Always seek the advice of your doctor or other qualified health professional with any questions you may have regarding surgery.

#6755. Julia Fox Pairs Her Preppy Purple Look With Her Vintage Prada Bowling Bag

The Y2K accessory is one of her favorites

When it comes to her style, Julia Fox has never been afraid to make a statement. Whether turning belts into a bra and miniskirt, tucking her hair into a “Velveeta gold” wig, or putting a futuristic twist on naked dressing, the actor has pushed the boundaries of red-carpet dressing and street style alike. Just this month, she showed up to the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards in a tulle Miss Claire Sullivan dress that was made using world maps.

For her latest look, Fox turned her attention to designer Sofia Testino, piecing together a preppy purple fit full of the label’s pieces. On top, she wore the Air Hostess Polo, a fitted short-sleeve ivory top with a purple collar and stripe of green. It featured an elongated button placket and a rounded deep V neckline trimmed in purple and green.

The Down the Drain memoir author wore the polo with the coordinating Air Hostess Arrow Skirt—a purple leather midi pencil skirt with arrow motifs across the front—and a reversible jacket. The Work Woman Jacket, as it is called, was crafted with a structured silhouette and padded shoulders. One side was cut from a cream checked wool, while the inverse was made from pale-blue satin with contrast checked panels. The whole thing was finished with brown ribbed cuffs.

Fox added semi-sheer black tights and periwinkle patent-leather wedges, also from Sofia Testino. And for a bag, she brought her go-to geometric black-and-white bowling bag from the Prada Spring/Summer 2000 collection.

#6754. Sean Duffy Is a Perfect Reality-TV Politician. Too Bad for America.

As a fellow MTV veteran, I recognize that the transportation secretary has been practicing for this moment since his Real World days.

Yesterday, in the wake of Friday’s grounding of all flights in the continental United States and the weekend of cancellations and chaos that ensued, Secretary of Transportation Sean Duffy made the rounds of the Sunday-morning political talk shows to make this face.

It’s been a tough few days for Duffy, and Duffy has been huffy. From Friday’s press conference with Markwayne Mullin, to his own microphonically challenged solo outing on Saturday, through all five morning-show appearances, Duffy has checked all the boxes the modern Republican official must: look aggrieved, place blame elsewhere, praise the president, be white. It’s all television, after all, and there’s a role to be played.

Sean Duffy has been at this for decades. You know he was on the sixth season of The Real World, and you know he met his wife and fellow Real Worlder Rachel Campos when they participated in an early season of what would become The Real World/Road Rules Challenge. But when you really go back and look, you’ll see that they’ve both been practicing for this moment since the ’90s. Game recognizes game; as a fellow alumnus of 20th-century MTV, let me walk you through it.

The Real World was born when MTV decided to try its hand at creating a weekly soap opera, in the style of the era-defining Beverly Hills 90210 and Melrose Place. But cable budgets being what they are, a full scripted show was untenable, so producers had the bold idea to do away with actors and writers entirely. The first couple seasons of The Real World kept the focus on art by casting young artists: Julie the emerging dancer amid the model and writer and singer-songwriters in season 1, Jon the wannabe Garth Brooks in season 2. But that went out the window quick, as producers and aspiring castmates figured out the same thing at the same time: The job was to stir the pot. So by season 3, deeply unpleasant bike messenger Puck was dropped into the San Francisco house to clash with saintly AIDS educator Pedro, with whom conservative Rachel initially refused to share a bathroom for fear of sink-based HIV transmission. In 1994.

By season 6, casts seemed chosen specifically to play a predetermined part and drive each other up the wall. Real World: Boston features Stanford med student Kameelah and ladies man Syrus, Catholic Texan Elka and Manhattan-born mess on wheels Montana. And then there was Sean Duffy, Wisconsin boy, lumberjack-games participant, and future secretary of transportation. Sean occupied the Cute Boy spot in the cast that had been held by Aaron in Los Angeles and St. Louis race car driver Mike in London. But this being 1997, he was also a different cultural archetype: the knee-jerk Rush Limbaugh–era conservative. Accent on jerk. Here’s a moment when Sean makes fun of Elka’s underwear and then immediately denies it.

Just straight-up, reflexively, smoothly denying he did something we just saw him do, because he did it on camera and into a microphone. Lying with a straight face. This would become a job skill.

It’s a little like something we saw Sean’s future wife Rachel do on her season. In an early episode, she engages in the hot, safe rebellious act of 1994 by getting her navel pierced. In a later episode, she gets a telephone call from her angry mother, who has seen the belly-button bauble in a photograph (a physical photograph, sent through the U.S. Mail—again, we lived differently in the ’90s). Rachel assures her mother it’s not a piercing but instead a sticker she put there. Forget the fact that, again, all of this is on camera and the mom is going to learn the truth soon enough. Watching back then, I remember thinking two things: One, this person lies very easily, and two, I think she might be one of those people who’s conservative just so they don’t upset their parents.

Anyway. Sean and Rachel did The Challenge and got married and had three hundred and fifty children. Sean became a congressman for Wisconsin; Rachel tried out for The View but never quite nailed it and now she’s on Fox & Friends sometimes. And because it all comes back to reality television and personal branding, the Duffy family spent a few weeks of Sean’s tenure as secretary of transportation to film a show. The Great American Road Trip was supposed to have aired on Fox Nation, but instead it got dumped onto YouTube, where most episodes are hovering around 10k views.

Also, Duffy’s son-in-law Michael Alfonso, who’s 26, is set to become the youngest person in Congress, having won a seat in Wisconsin’s 7th District in this month’s elections. Alfonso’s campaign was buoyed by generous campaign donations from numerous transportation companies, executives, and lobbyists, including a $2,500 contribution from Air Space Intelligence’s PAC in March. In June, the FAA awarded the AI start-up a contract worth up to $875.95 million. The company’s SMART system went nationwide days before the November 13 ground stop, though the FAA and the company said the outage was unrelated to SMART.

I’m sure it’s nothing. But I’m even more sure Duffy will say it is.

The way that John F. Kennedy understood television in the way Richard Nixon did not, Sean Duffy understands reality television in a way few others do. He’s lived it. He understands it as a marketing exercise. Knowing your brand, hitting the beats of the scene you’re supposed to play, showing up with a yearbook picture-day haircut and lying with a smile will get you far in this world. Whether he survives this turbulence or not, he’s learned that lesson.

Sean and Rachel have been at this for decades, but as viewers so have we. We can see them as they are: dishonest children, playing a role, begging for approval, just trying not to get yelled at.

#6753. Inside the New La Perla

La Perla’s comeback story isn’t a nostalgia play. CEO Alessio Vannetti recognizes that while people love vintage La Perla robes and bras, and past iconic brand moments like Halle Berry’s orange bikini in Die Another Day and Margot Robbie’s silk camisole in The Wolf of Wall Street, he’s on a mission to make modern-day La Perla just as famous.

“I want to do the best new lingerie ever. I want new generations looking at La Perla, not just for the vintage. I want the new generations purchasing totally brand new styles,” Vannetti says, speaking over Zoom from the brand’s Bologna, Italy, headquarters. “I think creativity should always lead.”

Vannetti, who became CEO of La Perla on September 1, is bullish on the lingerie brand’s prospects. Launched in 1954 by Ada Masotti, the brand became synonymous with luxury lingerie and was known for its handcrafted lacework, made by artisans in Bologna. By the 2010s, however, focus drifted as management changed hands. In 2023, La Perla was declared insolvent, and its factory went dormant. But its lace-trimmed lore lived on in pop culture: in the past few years, the brand got mentions in episodes of Emily in Paris and The Gentlemen, and Rosalía released a song called “La Perla” on her album Lux.

In 2025, La Perla was purchased by a white knight investor: entrepreneur and former Expedia CEO Peter Kern and his wife Kirsten swooped in to purchase the brand, its atelier and its former factory. They rehired all the artisans, and put a plan in place to bring La Perla back. That led to the hiring of Vannetti –– who has spent his career in brand and communications roles at houses like Gucci, Valentino, Zegna and Prada –– and a return to Milan Fashion Week with Collection #00 in September.

A new swimsuit collection will be shown later this month, to be available just in time for next summer. And La Perla’s e-commerce site is up and running, while Vannetti is scoping out revived wholesale partnerships with department stores and specialty boutiques, as well as potential store locations in the US, a top-priority market.

“Everything is new, and at the same time, everything is about reclaiming our original positioning,” says Vannetti. “How many times can you say that you are running a kind of startup, but with a brand equity as big as La Perla’s?

Where did La Perla go wrong? In part, Vannetti says, the company had pushed the brand to move faster than a high-luxury lingerie brand should. “My ambition is to create a profitable company, growing at the right speed, without compromising on quality or innovation,” says Vannetti. “In the first interview Ada Masotti gave when she founded the brand, she said that La Perla does not compromise on these two points. This is exactly what we are doing.”

Expansion at all costs is where compromise tends to happen. The brand is taking a slow approach: opening in new markets and with retail partners in order to contain that. Because lingerie is such a personal garment, he says, customers will immediately clock when the quality has gone down. “And they stop purchasing,” he says. “This is what happened in the past.”

La Perla’s production quality, after all, is its competitive edge. When the Kerns bought the company, they also bought the atelier and the factory, reviving the technique that Masotti created, on machines specific to La Perla. “She modified the machines in order to get a different stitch, and those modified machines are what have been preserved today,” Vannetti says. To keep that savoir-faire part of the brand’s secret sauce, La Perla will launch an academy in Bologna, where workers will train a new generation of sewers on techniques like frastaglio (where lace is inlaid on delicate silk or tulle) and macramé, as well as to maintain the machines should they need repair.

“We are not a public company, so we can really go at our speed, and our speed is not going to be extremely fast. It’s going to be La Perla speed,” Vannetti says. The brand has already designated the number of pieces it will produce for the next year, and it won’t exceed that. (He won’t say how many.) “I am 100% sure that by July, we will have the temptation. And that is the moment where I have to say, ‘OK, let’s stick to the plan.’ Because if you stick to the plan, and you build a brand on solid foundations, that’s where you’re healthy.”

Until then, the brand will test demand via its e-commerce store. Over the summer, the brand invited more than 200,000 former clients to shop the new collection on a private e-commerce platform ahead of the full dot-com relaunch in October. Bras started selling out, and Vannetti says a brand might be tempted to refill stock to capture sales that would otherwise go unfulfilled by switching production to a shorter-lead model — three weeks, say, rather than La Perla’s typical three months.

The brand is also testing the waters via one retail partnership with Harrods. The brand hosted a trunk show at Harrods for 19 clients — “the revenue generated was astonishing,” Vannetti says, though, again, he won’t disclose any figures as it’s too soon after a three-year shutdown. Now, he says, Harrods is pushing the brand to reopen its concession with the store. “We are running after that; we are trying to be as fast as we can, but that is the power of a desirable brand — the feeling that clients are missing it.”

Stores are also part of the strategic plan, though Vannetti says it will only open in specific markets, starting with New York and Los Angeles. The brand once had 135 stores at its peak. “We will never be in that situation again.”

While the history of the brand is paramount to this new phase, Vannetti is adamant the future of La Perla is not steeped in its past. “We all want to stay away from nostalgia,” he says. “When memories become nostalgia, there is the feeling that you cannot create new memories.”

What will the new cultural touchpoints for La Perla be? Vannetti says he is in conversations with Hollywood stylists and studios to get celebrities wearing La Perla or to have La Perla mentioned in more shows à la Emily in Paris. He’s also considering collaborations with other brands or designers if it feels right. Newness is part of the plan: in the next year, 60% of revenue will come from the brand’s icons, or recurring pieces, while 35% will be from new or one-off products.

The brand is also benefiting from the halo effect of the bra trend that dominated runways during the Spring/Summer 2027 season, perfectly in sync with its return. “Our mantra is inside by tradition, outside by choice, which is basically saying that we don’t want to and we will never become a ready-to-wear brand. But today, the bra is becoming the new top.”

But don’t expect La Perla to fully embrace ready-to-wear. That’s another wrong step past ownership took, Vannetti says. “They went in the direction of trying to aggressively become a ready-to-wear brand.” It worked best when it partnered with designers like Jean-Paul Gaultier or Dell’Acqua. “Those two moments were very good examples of how to preserve the brand identity. When they tried to expand to many different categories, it didn’t work. I really just want to do the best lingerie ever. It’s kind of the art of lingerie, the way we are approaching the future.”

#6752. An Inside Guide to Kate Middleton’s Royal Tour Wardrobe in India

India had sat at the top of Kate Middleton’s bucket list ever since she married into the royal family in 2011. Or so said Prince William during their 2016 tour of the subcontinent, for which the then Duchess of Cambridge packed a wardrobe that brought together the fashion heritage of both India and the country she represented.

First came an Alexander McQueen dress, its paisley print drawing on the textile traditions of India and Persia, followed by a pink-and-pistachio tunic dress by Mumbai-based designer Anita Dongre, worn to play cricket at the Oval Maidan with children supported by local charities. Then along came a Jenny Packham gown, hand-beaded by Indian artisans, to a Bollywood gala, and a blue-and-white embroidered sheath dress by Cumballa Hill-born designer Naeem Khan for a visit to the Taj Mahal. Together, the looks offered an early indication of Kate’s understanding of clothing as a diplomatic tool.

Catherine, the Princess of Wales, wearing Alexander McQueen at the Taj Hotel in Mumbai, 2016.

Catherine, the Princess of Wales, at the Oval Maidan in Mumbai, 2016.

A decade on, the couple returned to India this morning, where Prince William is set to host the sixth Earthshot Prize summit and awards ceremony in Mumbai. The annual Earthshot Prize gives £1 million ($1.3 million) each to five winners tackling some of the world’s most pressing environmental problems, and India has produced more finalists than any other country since its founding in 2020. It will be Catherine’s first appearance at the event since Boston in 2022, while the inaugural Children’s Earthshot Awards should provide familiar territory for the princess, whose charitable work has long focused on the next generation.

Beyond the prizes, though, the Prince and Princess will undertake a series of engagements across Mumbai (and New Delhi), highlighting the cultural ties between Britain and India, including cricket, film, and, of course, fashion. Will Kate turn to favorite brand Edeline Lee (whose signature bubble-flou fabric can be scrunched into a suitcase and spring back to life without a crease), or perhaps one of the directional Indian designers spotlighted in British Vogue’s July 2026 issue? Lovebirds and Raw Mango, for instance, have both presented collections at London Fashion Week in recent seasons.

#6744. The Guest in Residence x Moon Boot Collab Is Back for Round Two

Cashmere sweaters and leather jackets may be in full rotation right now, but with snow season just a whisper away, we’ll all be swapping for puffer coats and thick socks before we know it. Usually that also means pulling out chunky boots that do more for warmth than they do for an outfit. But Gigi Hadid’s Guest in Residence and Moon Boot are here to make finding non-hideous winter boots a lot easier. Launching November 17, the two brands will release a second collaboration of five special-edition boots, each one pairing Guest in Residence’s colorful hand-knit textures with Moon Boot’s cushy soles and weatherproof materials, fully built for any chilly-day adventure—in the city or the mountains.

The two brands are putting forth the notion that a snow boot can look just as good as your favorite sweater, and this round further homes in on that idea. “Each silhouette tells a story that feels nostalgic and familiar yet entirely new at the same time,” Hadid shares. The new styles hark back to vintage themes—think alpine motifs and old cabin textiles. The Icon Knit Postcard features a custom knit landscape of snowy pines that looks like a walk through the woods and comes in an ice and red colorway or dark brown and sand. The Nolace Fair Isle is low-profile and pairs a classic Fair Isle pattern with a fuzzy fleece texture. For something more pared back, the Icon Low Signature comes in suede with pastel laces, blue on the pale pair and pink on the brown. The Ltrack Cable Knit adds wool and alpaca cable-knit. Every style has its own playful vibe.

Hadid started Guest in Residence in 2022 and it quickly became the place to get really good colorful cashmere sweaters. She’s also been wearing Moon Boots for years, so teaming up with the brand was a no-brainer. “We’ve brought our vibrant palettes and retro influences to Moon Boot’s futuristic silhouettes, and it’s been fun to explore new expressions of our partnership,” Hadid says.

And no, you don’t need to be a prolific skier to put them to use. Built on Moon Boot’s signature soles, they’re as useful on a slick sidewalk as they are on the slopes. Moon Boot itself was never strictly for the snow either. It started in 1969 as an après-ski boot inspired by what the astronauts wore on the moon. If you are venturing out to the slopes this season, Guest in Residence just released an eight-piece ski capsule with Halfdays, the label cofounded by former Olympic freestyle skier Kiley McKinnon, so you can match your boots to your bib. The line is featured within the brands’ joint campaign, shot in faux airport terminals and cabins, with all manner of cable knits, cozy thermals, and necessary footwear for a trip to the mountains.

Whether you’re looking for a statement shoe to complete your ski suit or a low-profile ankle boot to polish off your winter wares, there’s a pair that fits the bill in the collaboration. Prices range from $395 to $495, and the collection is available starting November 17 at Moon Boot and Guest in Residence. Vogue readers can shop the drop exclusively 24 hours before the rest on Vogue Shopping.

#6743. The Stars Brought the Glamour to the 2026 Baby2Baby Gala Presented by Paul Mitchell

Harper’s Bazaar has exclusive snapshots from the event’s portrait studio

Every year, the Baby2Baby Gala brings together the biggest names in philanthropy, entertainment, and fashion for an evening of fundraising and celebration. And last night, the 15th edition was no exception—with the likes of Demi Moore, Andrew Garfield, Jessica Alba, Barbara Palvin and Dylan Sprouse, Kerry Washington, and Katy Perry showing up in West Hollywood to support the organization’s mission to help kids in need.

From the gala’s inception, Baby2Baby co-CEOS Norah Weinstein and Kelly Sawyer Patricof wanted to create an event that felt “less like a traditional fundraiser.” Year after year, the night has offered its star-studded guests more than just an excuse to get dressed up and a chance to make a difference with their wallets—pairing fundraising efforts with exclusive dining experiences from top chefs and intimate performances from award-winning musicians. (Last night, Carbone flew in from New York City and Cardi B took the stage.)

“We wanted to create an event that would appeal to a younger generation of donors through our approach to food, fashion, and celebrity, while making sure that nobody left the gala without understanding our mission,” Weinstein and Patricof told Harper’s Bazaar. “Fifteen years later, the gala has evolved in ways we never could have imagined, but some themes have stayed exactly the same. Every flower and fork is underwritten by our generous sponsors so that every dollar raised can go directly to Baby2Baby and the children we serve.”

Each year, the funds raised from the gala help get millions of basic essentials—from diapers to formula to clothing—to children who need them. But the organization, with an all-female board of directors, spends all year working towards the Baby2Baby mission. “The gala brings this extraordinary community together for one night, but it’s their commitment throughout the year that makes our work possible,” the CEOs shared.

#6742. What Is Outercourse? Everything to Know About Sex Without Penetration

Because penetration isn't the only way to have really good sex (or really good orgasms).

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Blame it on the sexy movies, erotic books, or yeah—fine—porn, but at some point, we collectively decided that penetration was the main event. Not sure why (because, hi, have you ever received oral sex?), but everything else has sort of turned into “something to do until we get there,” and the “there” in question is penetrative sex.

Which is a pretty unfortunate way to think about sex, considering how much good stuff happens without penetration ever entering the picture.

Enter: outercourse. Think making out, dry humping, mutual masturbation, and all the other ways you can get off (or just get really turned on) without penetration. And no, these activities aren't just consolation prizes for when intercourse isn't happening. They can be the entire experience, orgasms and all.

The term gained popularity during the safer-sex movement of the 1980s, explains Good Vibrations sexologist Carol Queen, PhD, author of Exhibitionism for the Shy. It was developed “to help people differentiate between intercourse and all the sexy things that can be enjoyed without penetration,” she says.

Whether you're avoiding penetration, looking to switch things up with a partner, or just realizing you've been skipping over some genuinely excellent options, outercourse deserves a little more attention. Ahead, everything to know about what counts as outercourse, why it's worth exploring, and how to make the most of it.

Outercourse is an umbrella term for sexual activities that don't involve penetration, although the exact definition can vary. “The details of what this means is subjective from person to person based on their boundaries,” says Tyomi Morgan, certified sexologist and pleasure coach. “Some people consider outercourse everything but penis in vagina. Others exclude penetration of any kind including fingers, sex toys, and anal sex.”

Basically, outercourse can include everything from making out and sensual massages to dry humping, external sex toy play, and certain BDSM activities, says Queen.

“Many people think of outercourse as pretty vanilla, but depending on what you like, it can include as many slaps or spanks as light-fingered strokes. It is truly a variable type of play and very much worth exploring.”

And just because penetration isn't involved doesn't mean orgasms are off the table. Outercourse can be just as pleasurable as intercourse (if not more so...), and taking penetration out of the equation might even help you discover new ways to get off.

The two terms are sometimes used interchangeably, but there's an important distinction. “I am not crazy about the term 'foreplay,' because it implies that you are doing activities that precede the real main event,” says Queen.

The thing is, plenty of people have great sex without ever having intercourse. As Queen puts it, “Penetration is an option, not the definition of sex.”

Calling something outercourse rather than foreplay acknowledges that it can be the entire sexual experience, not just a lead-up to penetration. There's no requirement for anything to come before or after it.

There are plenty of reasons to explore outercourse, whether you're looking to switch things up or simply aren't interested in penetration. Here are a few benefits Queen and Morgan highlight:

Beyond the physical benefits, outercourse can also be a way to explore new forms of intimacy and connection with a partner. “Outercourse can be just as satisfying as penetration (if not more so),” reminds Morgan.

Generally speaking, outercourse can carry a lower risk of pregnancy and certain sexually transmitted infections (STIs) than penetrative sex, but that doesn't mean it's completely risk-free. The level of risk depends on what you're doing, whether you're using protection, and the type of contact involved.

For example, massages and touching over clothing generally carry little to no STI risk, while kissing, oral sex, and direct genital contact can transmit certain infections. Some STIs, including herpes and HPV, can spread through skin-to-skin contact, even without penetration. And while outercourse can reduce pregnancy risk, pregnancy is still possible if semen gets on or near the vaginal opening.

Talking with your partner about STI testing, using condoms or dental dams when appropriate, and practicing good hygiene can help reduce potential risks.

And, of course, consent is just as important during outercourse as it is during penetrative sex. “It's no less important to make sure your partner wants to do the things you're planning to do when engaging in external touch,” says Queen.

The easiest way to explore outercourse? Start doing more of the things you might normally rush through on your way to penetration. Take your time making out, explore different kinds of touch, and don't feel like you need to move straight to genital stimulation. Talk about your fantasies, give each other sensual massages, or see if you can bring each other to orgasm without penetration, recommends Queen.

And if you're looking to build even more anticipation, consider edging. “Try edging,” suggests sexologist Shamyra Howard, author of Use Your Mouth. “During mutual masturbation, attempt to bring each other to orgasm but stop right before orgasm occurs. Repeat this at least three times for an intense, mind-blowing climax.”

Ultimately, there's no right or wrong way to explore outercourse, as long as everyone involved is comfortable and consenting. Whether it becomes a regular part of your sex life or something you turn to when you're not in the mood for penetration, the point is to enjoy the experience without treating intercourse as the inevitable next step.

#6740. The Best Black Bags Are a Worthwhile Investment

The best black bags are, above all else, wardrobe necessities. Just like a good pair of jeans or a crisp white T-shirt, they’re the kind of accessories that never feel out of place, seamlessly working just about anything in your closet. Having a few different styles to rotate depending on the occasion just makes sense.

Tote bags and roomy bucket bags are ideal for office days, when carrying your laptop and other commuter essentials is a must, while shoulder bags offer a streamlined option for when you want to travel a little lighter. Crossbody bags offer a hands-free approach, while top handles give instant polish on the go, tapping into ladylike sensibilities that can make even the simplest outfit feel a little more pulled together. And for evenings out, party bags bring just the right amount of occasion-worthy appeal, with playful accents and embellishments that make getting dressed feel a little more fun.

If you’re in the market for a black handbag, consider this your guide to the very best styles available. From iconic shapes like Bottega Veneta’s Andiamo woven bag to Gucci’s reimagined Jackie shoulder bag, these are a few of the investment-worthy luxury designs that will earn their keep in your wardrobe for years to come. But you also don’t have to spend a fortune to find a great option: Coach’s offerings strike the perfect balance between timeless design, quality, and affordability.

Ahead, browse Vogue’s edit of the best black handbags to buy and cherish.

Ideal for office days, when carrying your laptop and other commuter essentials is a must, tote bags are the hardest-working styles in any handbag lineup. Choose one with staying power that can go the distance. From Bottega Veneta’s woven leather Andiamo and Dragon Diffusion’s large Santa Croce style to The Row’s Clement and Coach’s roomy Brooklyn shopper (a favorite of Bella Hadid), these carryalls make a case for investing in a black work bag you’ll reach for day after day.

When traveling a little lighter, black shoulder bags offer a streamlined option that still leaves room for the essentials—and looks great while doing it. Whether you’re drawn to Gucci’s reimagined Jackie shoulder bag (a celebrity favorite), Staud’s minimalist-friendly Strada, or Alaïa’s sculptural Click, think of these as silhouettes that can take you from daytime meetings to evenings out with ease.

Reach for a top-handle bag for instant polish on the go. With their ladylike sensibilities, these classic styles make even the simplest outfit feel a little more pulled together, especially in timeless black. Prada’s Passage shot straight to the top of our wish lists for its sleek silhouette, while Savette’s Symmetry is the editor-approved ‘IYKYK’ choice, each one of these luxury bags striking the sweet spot between timeless appeal and modern design.

For a hands-free approach, crossbody bags do the job and then some. Whether you’re commuting, traveling, running weekend errands, or tackling everything in between, what can’t these black bags do? Some of our favorites include Khaite’s Blake, Toteme’s T-lock (it’s convertible!), and Demellier’s Vancouver—all versatile options that offer plenty of styling mileage.

Party bags are the category where you can experiment a little more. Although still versatile because of their color, they’ll bring something playful to the table that your everyday handbag cannot. Sleek satin clutches, embellished pouches, and velvet wristlets (even something in suede!) all add an instant dose of personality to even the simplest going-out look, thanks to a new wave of contemporary designs from Liffner, Sophie Buhai, and Valesque.

#6738. 10 Different Types of Sex You Should Know About (and Definitely Explore)

Because there's a whole lot more to sex than penetration.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

For something people talk about, think about, and Google pretty much constantly, sex has a surprisingly narrow reputation. Say the word, and chances are your brain immediately jumps to penetration. Which, sure, can be great. But treating intercourse as the default definition of sex leaves out a whole lot of ways people experience pleasure.

“Sex is a mental, physical, spiritual, and emotional erotic connection shared between those involved,” explains sexologist Shamyra Howard, author of Use Your Mouth. “It's not just about genitals and mouths.”

As psychotherapist Kristie Overstreet, PhD, puts it, “there isn't one that is ‘better’ or a ‘more real’ type of sex than the other.”

Ultimately, what matters is that everyone involved is on the same page about boundaries, expectations, and consent. For people who are into foot worship, feet can be a major source of arousal. “Just as other parts of the body are turn-ons, for some, feet are the turn-on,” says Overstreet.

Think kissing, cuddling, body rubbing, mutual masturbation, dry humping, boob sex (rubbing a penis between breasts), and discussing each other's fantasies.

These toys can be anything from wand vibrators to dildos to butt plugs to rabbit vibrators, and they can be used alone or with a partner. #6737. The 11 Best Airbnbs in Switzerland Offer More Than Just Alpine Views

There are few places as beautiful as Switzerland, with its dramatic Alpine peaks, wildflower-dotted meadows, and glacier-fed lakes. Few countries are as easy to explore, either, thanks to a superb public transportation system that makes reaching even the most remote hiking trails a scenic train or bus ride away.

Switzerland is also, of course, famously expensive. Though its hospitality industry is world-renowned, to experience the country at a different pace—and perhaps even get a more intimate experience of what it’s like to call one of its quintessential Alpine villages home—consider booking an Airbnb. Whether you’re planning a ski holiday with a group or getting a head start on a romantic summer escape for two, these vacation rentals offer a chance to experience Switzerland’s beauty up close.

From mountain chalets and cozy cabins to lakeside villas and city condos, here are 11 Airbnbs in Switzerland to book this winter.

The views are almost too beautiful to believe at this two-bedroom apartment. Whether you’re gazing out on the glacial-clear Lake Thun from the bedroom, or meditating on the Alps (the visible peaks here include Eiger, Mönch and Jungfrau) from the wrap-around balcony, everywhere you look is a feast for the senses.

Nestled in the Alpine village of Beatenberg, here, you’re not far from Interlaken and its renowned snow sports. The home has been recently renovated and marries the charm of its surroundings with the comforts of modern living, including a contemporary kitchen stocked with cooking basics as well as a raclette machine and fondue pot. If you’re visiting in the summer, hiking trails leave from the front door.

Bern, Switzerland’s capital city, is often overshadowed by the country’s glitzier towns and larger cities, but there’s so much here to enjoy. This sun-drenched one-bedroom apartment makes an ideal base for exploring it all. Offering enough space and amenities to feel like home, it’s particularly well suited for a longer stay: There’s a generous dining table that can accommodate a crowd, a dedicated workspace, and speedy internet.

From here, it’s a 15-minute walk from the UNESCO-listed Old Town and its many splendors—including the medieval Zytglogge, the famous Bear Park, the Aare River, and one of the country’s best-known Michelin restaurants, Wein & Sein. The superb Zentrum Paul Klee museum is also just a short tram ride away.

The minimal, open floor plan and neutral palette of this chalet lets its views of Lake Thun take center stage. Set directly on the shores of the lake, the chalet’s balcony gives visitors a front-row seat to the view; you would be forgiven for spending all of your time there by the outdoor fireplace.

While it’s tempting to lounge the days away, the draw of nearby ski resorts makes this an ideal spot for travelers seeking adventure. The home is particularly ideal for families, thanks to a toddler bed, crib, and optional second apartment within the chalet available to rent for larger or multi-generational groups. While family-friendly ski resorts are very close by, the larger towns of Thun and Interlaken are also very easy to reach from this perch.

Set in the heart of the village of Scuol, directly opposite the town’s central fountain, the property offers a glimpse of idyllic Alpine life, with the Swiss National Park just beyond its doorstep. It’s a wonderfully peaceful setting, yet only an hour from the glitz and glamour of St. Moritz.

Floor-to-ceiling windows and a contemporary palette of soft neutrals blur the line between the indoors and outdoors. This is an ideal retreat for those drawn to the water (and therefore, perhaps better-suited for warmer weather), with complimentary paddleboards at the ready and easy access to swimming. When you’re ready to venture further afield, the fairy tale Hünegg Castle is a short walk and the historic town of Thun a 15-minute drive away.

The appeal extends well beyond its superb setting, however. An airy open floor plan living space is anchored by a striking hanging fireplace, while the three bedrooms each come with their own en-suite bathroom, making this an ideal spot for groups of couples or friends. (Speaking of: Both the Odeon and the Kunsthaus are about a 20-minute walk away.)

Built in 1900 in a traditional Engadine style with thick stone walls and a sloping Dutch roof, the two-bedroom apartment feels plucked from the pages of a story book. At its heart is a Stube-style room anchored by a massive fireplace and filled with traditional furniture, sheepskin rugs, and oil landscapes.

In the kitchen, you’ll find both cowbells and a fondue pot—fitting reminders of where you are. Although you may be tempted to end each day on the balcony enjoying the views of the Engadine Valley and Alps, here, you’re not far from the town’s famous après-ski scene, which pulses around Badrutt’s Palace and Chesa Veglia. #6735. Cream Cheese Mashed Potatoes

Cream cheese doesn't just belong on bagels. In this instance, it's the secret to making mashed potatoes even creamier, richer, and more decadent. The high fat content in the cream cheese melds with the butter and milk to make the potatoes perfectly fluffy and creamy. Serve these at your Thanksgiving or Christmas dinner, or even on a cozy weeknight alongside oven-baked pork chops.

What People Are Saying:“Never cooked mashed potatoes before, and this recipe turned out amazing.” - GreenHat

“Silky smooth and delicious” - BlueProcessor

In a large pot, cover the potatoes with water and season with salt. Bring it to a boil and cook until the potatoes are softened and fork-tender, which should take about 16 to 18 minutes. Drain and return the potatoes to the pot.

Add cream cheese and, using a potato masher, mash potatoes until smooth.

Meanwhile, in a small saucepan over medium heat, cook the milk and butter. Stir until the butter is melted, which should take about 2 minutes. Pour the butter mixture over the potatoes.

Stir until it's combined and creamy. Make sure to season with salt and pepper!

Transfer potatoes to a serving bowl and season with more pepper. Top with chives and enjoy!

The full list of ingredients and instructions can be found in the recipe below.

Store leftover cream cheese mashed potatoes in an airtight container in the refrigerator for up to 4 days.

#6734. All the Red-Carpet Looks From the 2026 Governors Awards

From Emma Stone to Zendaya and Charlize Theron

The Governors Awards are back. The annual gala hosted by the Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences (who also hosts the Oscars) to honor lifetime achievement and extraordinary contributions to film has returned for its 17th edition—and the red carpet is brimming with star power.

While this gala itself is not aired live, like the main Oscars ceremony (which takes place in March every year), the awards given are considered genuine Oscars. Until 2009, the awards now given at the Governors Awards were presented live during the main Oscars telecast, but the Academy separated them into their own gala—both to shorten the main television broadcast and to give honorees more time to celebrate their wins and present their speeches.

Last year, Tom Cruise, Debbie Allen, and Wynn Thomas received Honorary Oscars, while Dolly Parton was given the Jean Hersholt Humanitarian Award. On the red carpet, we saw everyone, from Jennifer Lawrence to Anya Taylor-Joy, Cynthia Erivo, and Dakota Johnson.

Meanwhile, tonight’s honorees include Glenn Close, Ridley Scott, and Floyd Norman—who are all receiving Honorary Oscars—alongside producers Christine Vachon and Pamela Koffler, who will receive the Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award.

But there are plenty of other guests in attendance this evening at the Ray Dolby Ballroom at Ovation Hollywood in Los Angeles—from Penélope Cruz in Chanel to Zendaya in Gilles Deacon, Charlize Theron in Tom Ford, Tessa Thompson in Schiaparelli, and Emma Stone in Louis Vuitton.

#6733. 28 Black-Tie Wedding Guest Dresses That Are Perfectly Formal

So you’ve been invited to a black-tie wedding. Before descending into a formalwear-induced anxiety spiral, consider the benefits—this is your chance to go all out with a statement evening look you’d never otherwise get the chance to wear. (Plus, the people-watching is guaranteed to be incredible.)

The main thing to keep in mind is the definition of the dress code. Put simply, “black tie” means floor-length hemlines, so leave the midi dress at home in favor of something ankle-sweeping. From there, you’ll find there’s a lot of variation within the black-tie wedding guest dress options.

If you’re stuck on where to start, begin by considering the setting. A black-tie event in an upscale city venue requires a different sort of formal dress than a black-tie event in a bucolic setting. Think about fabrics and the season, as well as silhouette: Do you go light and dreamy, with layers of tulle and pleated chiffon? Or moody and sophisticated with luxe silk and embroidered jacquard? When in doubt, a classic A-line or halterneck style offers just enough formality. And for winter weddings, anything in rich tones, from golden saffron to oxblood to inky navy, is a safe bet.

From simple black dresses to beaded sequins, shop our edit of the best black-tie wedding guest dresses, ahead.

You can’t go wrong with a satin dress. Elegant and polished, these slinky gowns feel like the ideal canvas for all sorts of accessorizing. SIR’s short-sleeve dark plum dress calls for minimalism with sculptural gold jewelry; something sleeker, such as Amsale’s liquid gold gown, is an opportunity to play with semi-precious gemstone jewelry (like amethysts, citrines, tourmalines, and aquamarines) to enhance the richness of the hue.

Come winter, velvet is hard to beat. Its plush texture and light-catching sheen add instant depth to a simple silhouette, as seen with Bernadette’s embellished column dress and Liberowe’s halter maxi. Just be sure to pair your look with minimal jewelry to let the fabric take the lead.

It’s in the name: If you’re not sure what to wear for this dress code, black is generally a safe bet. Experiment with the shape to bring interest to a restrained palette. Norma Kamali’s gown, which has a plunging cowl back, and Staud’s blouson bodice dress, which has a cinched waist tie, are both good options; then, use your accessories to bring personality to your look.

Take inspiration from the champagne toast with your black-tie gown. Solace London provides a relaxed fit with lots of shine, while Ralph Lauren’s silver style is great for those who prefer something a little less flashy. If you’re searching for after-hours glamour, look no further than Aje’s feather-trimmed sequined gown or Taller Marmo’s radiant gold mockneck.

There’s something especially sculptural about a draped dress. Whether it’s expressed throughout the silhouette, as on Tory Burch’s design, or shows up in the one-shoulder asymmetry of Simkhai’s dark spruce gown, the fluid finish feels undeniably artistic.

No one said long-sleeve formal dresses had to be frumpy. Try a sleeker silhouette, like Victoria Beckham’s black twist-waist gown or Kallmeyer’s minimalist burgundy maxi. For a looser fit, Proenza Schouler’s cold-shoulder piece with an open back is just what you need.

Need proof that floral dresses aren’t just for garden weddings? Consider, if you will, the following: burgundy versions from Rodarte and A.L.C., a moody but romantic ruffled option from Rixo, and an elegant floral-cloqué gown courtesy of Emilia Wickstead.

Can I wear colors other than black to a black-tie wedding?

Yes! This dress code has more to do with length and style than color—and while wearing a black gown is certainly a shortcut to looking put-together, sometimes your host will actually prefer color. A summer destination wedding is the perfect occasion for bright hues, for instance, while a fall event calls for a moody, autumnal palette.

Can I wear a jumpsuit instead of a dress?

It all depends on the jumpsuit. Something in a luxe fabric, like silk or taffeta, with sharp tailoring is probably alright (though to be safe, you can always double-check with the couple). And if you’re unsure, the staunchly anti-gown guest can opt for a polished suit in similarly upscale materials for a sophisticated twist on the dress code.

Should I choose my black-tie dress depending on the venue or season?

When in doubt, take context clues from the invite. The season, venue, and even time of ceremony are all helpful indicators of what kind of dress to pick—you wouldn’t wear a velvet floor-length gown to a morning ceremony in July, just as a strapless style in floral crepe might be a poor choice for a snowy winter fête.

What are the key guidelines for black-tie wedding guest dresses?

Can a wedding guest wear a midi dress to a black-tie wedding?

Absolutely not. Black tie equals floor-length hemlines, and a midi will look out of place in a room full of gowns.

What are the differences between a black-tie and a black-tie optional wedding guest dress?

Black-tie optional gives you more flexibility with your eveningwear, whereas black tie is stricter about what you can wear. In other words, a sophisticated midi is acceptable at a black-tie optional wedding, though a gown is still your best choice. (Minis, on the other hand, are always off-limits.)

That was so unprofessional not only that, but why are you acting like the semifinals aren’t on Tuesday??? Just handing 9s to whoever or whatever the dance was.”

Another person even joked that Coach Jackie should have been in the ballroom to “keep eyes on Derek.”

“What do you actually mean derek was ‘too tired to judge’ so he just gave everybody a nine,” they wrote alongside a clip of Jackie in the DWTS audience a few weeks prior.

What’s more: Derek retroactively shared a vlog recounting the day he got “three hours of sleep.”

“So this is why you were funky during the show 😭,” someone commented, while another criticized, “Next year, maybe delay the tour so that the contestants can be fairly judged by someone who isn’t just throwing in the towel and scoring all 9s because they’re tired.”

Derek has yet to comment further on the matter, if at all, but we’ll see what he brings to the judging panel during the semifinals on Tuesday!

#6727. In Trans Communities Across the Country, Clothing Swaps Are Promoting Style—and Safety

Every time Isaiah Kirk leaves the Uncommon Closet’s clothing swap, he steps out onto the streets of Chicago in a new look. Most recently, his haul included a pair of jeans matched with a Pokémon graphic tee and a button-up shirt. The total cost of this outfit? $0.00.

"It’s a lot of fun," says Kirk, who identifies as nonbinary and transmasculine, of sourcing new wardrobe items at clothing swaps like the one held by the Uncommon Closet. “The environment, the camaraderie, it’s safe and inclusive.”

For Kirk and other trans and queer people, clothing isn’t just about style; it’s about expressing themselves in a way that aligns with their gender identity and feeling as comfortable as possible in their own skin, even as anti-trans legislation continues to threaten or limit trans existence in public life across the United States.

A clothing swap setup at Chicago’s Uncommon Closet.

“I primarily work with people who have body dysmorphia or dysphoria,” says AJ Mix, who runs community outreach at the Uncommon Closet. Many of their clients are trans or queer people who have faced discrimination for expressing a different gender identity than their sex assigned at birth. “A lot of people have trauma going to stores,” Mix says. Their job is often to function as a sort of therapeutic personal stylist, accompanying clients—some of whom are trans women early in their transition—on trips to gendered shopping aisles they might be afraid to navigate alone.

According to Mix, the Uncommon Closet’s clothing swap was meant to be a one-time event in 2024, during the queer sewing and alteration business’s grand reopening. But then they noticed the huge demand for this free service. Mix now hosts three swaps a year—in spring, summer, and winter—to help people prepare for the seasonal shifts in Chicago’s weather. “We have a lot of new people coming in from the South to Chicago due to the political climate,” Mix says. “A lot of people use their entire savings to haul their entire lives up here, and so they just don’t have any money.”

Elsewhere, on the East Coast, Alex Carroll, the 20-year-old founder of NYC Youth for Trans Rights, organizes clothing swaps for young people who struggle to afford gender-affirming accessories. A binder, which flattens the chest to give wearers a more masculine silhouette, can run upwards of $50 and be difficult to source in person. Carroll loves to see people walk away from their clothing swaps, visibly thrilled to have scored a binder for free.

But even more than that, Carroll loves seeing people make new friends and find their community at these meetups. “There’s constantly stuff being thrown at trans people,” Carroll says. “You have to find people who are similar to you to help support each other.”

In Austin, Christabell Nunez wanted to create a safe space for marginalized people of all different body sizes to explore fashion. “It is still so hard to shop for plus-sized people. You really have to scavenge,” Nunez says. “A lot of our trans people, they are getting stared at. They’re getting looked at in retail spaces.”

In 2024, Nunez hosted a plus-sized clothing swap in her backyard that was attended by 20 friends. Now that work has expanded into the nonprofit I Slay, which hosts swaps for well over 150 people every month. Everyone is welcome to take as much as they want, Nunez says; the only limitation is that people are asked not to resell the clothes for profit.

“Our mind gets blown every month,” Nunez says. “There’s somebody who flew in from Canada one time to come to our swap.”

Melissa Grzybowski, head librarian of youth services at the Pflugerville Public Library in central Texas, says the trans clothing-swap program held by the library doesn’t just help the trans community: “It helps everyone.” At one of the library’s first swaps, Grzybowski saw a refugee mother find new clothes for her children after fleeing their country with only two suitcases of belongings.

In 2021, Texas proposed the highest number of anti-trans laws in the United States. Still, Grzybowski tries to make her programming and literature inclusive. “Public libraries are supposed to be welcoming to all people,” she says. “We have lots of books that represent the LGBTQ+ experience.”

A clothing swap attendee browses at Tennessee’s Trans Empowerment Project.

One state over, in Tennessee, Jack Knoxville saw very little representation for trans people when he started a clothing swap back in 2016. At the time, he felt there were only negative representations and ideas of what it meant to be trans in his home state.

“I’ve been on my own and dealt with being unhoused from the age of 14 on, and had to deal with surviving so many things,” Knoxville says. “I know what it’s like to be trying so hard to get a job and getting the door shut in your face because your name on paper doesn’t match the perception of your gender with that interviewer.”

Knoxville says they’ve seen people walk out of their clothing swaps feeling more confident for job interviews and equipped for the cold winter while couch surfing. “It’s the same kind of euphoria that you feel when you have a good haircut… You can feel like you’re on top of the world,” Knoxville says. “Clothing has a huge impact on people and their ability to interact with each other, but then on top of it is that layer of safety and how other people perceive you.”

Now, with the nonprofit Trans Empowerment Project, Knoxville has helped to show people from across the country how to host clothing swaps in their own cities.

“Queer people especially have to be resilient, and they have to tap into creativity and create something from nothing,” Knoxville says. “We find ways to take what’s left, the crumbs that are given to us, and turn them into celebrations.”

#6726. Turkey Cheese Ball

The turkey may be the main event of Thanksgiving dinner, but it can be a snack, too! Whether you're hosting Thanksgiving for your community or celebrating with family, this fun appetizer is the perfect way to get everyone excited for the big turkey dinner with another smaller turkey! This cheese ball is not only the most eye-catching appetizer you can add to your holiday menu, but it’s surprisingly easy to create.

What People Are Saying:“I made this for Friendsgiving. It was delicious and I got several comments on how great it tasted.” - kellykcox34580

In a food processor, pulse the cream cheese, goat cheese, butter, and garlic powder until the mixture is smooth. Add parsley, chives, and thyme and pulse until well combined. Then season with salt and black pepper.

Transfer the cream cheese mixture to a piece of plastic wrap and gather up the wrap to form a ball. Then transfer the mixture into a large bowl and refrigerate until it’s firm, which should take at least three hours and up to one day.

In a clean food processor, pulse 3/4 cup pecan halves until they are finely chopped.

Roll the cheese ball in nuts and arrange it all on a platter.

Start by cutting a large triangle from a red bell pepper—this will be the face. Next, cut a smaller triangle from a yellow bell pepper for the beak. For the eyes, slice two small rounds from a small carrot. Take a larger carrot and slice it into diagonal ovals to make the feet, then carve little triangles at one end to look like toes. Finally, thinly slice the rest of the carrots into matchsticks for the tail.

Grab some toothpicks and stick the carrot eyes onto the red triangle face. Then, use another toothpick to add the yellow beak to the face—just make sure all the toothpicks point toward the back so you can attach everything to the cheese ball later. Now, press the face onto the front of the cheese ball, and slide the carrot feet into the base.

Insert matchstick carrots, pretzel sticks, and mini pretzels into the back of the ball to create the tail. Using the remaining 1/2 cup pecan halves, create wings by overlapping them along each side of the ball. Serve with crackers alongside!

The full list of ingredients and instructions can be found in the recipe below.

Store leftover cheese ball in an airtight container in the refrigerator for up to 4 days. If possible, remove the pretzels and vegetable decorations before storing, as they can soften over time.

#6725. Robert Pattinson Is the Next James Bond! Here’s Who He Beat Out for the Job

It’s official: Robert Pattinson is the next James Bond. After months of speculation, it has finally been confirmed that the 40-year-old Brit will slip into 007’s tux.

Probably the most high-profile name on the list of likely contenders, Pattinson may seem, to many, a more logical choice for a Bond villain than 007 himself, but I’d already argued he’d make an excellent franchise lead: The Batman shows that he can be a convincing action hero, and with his weird and wonderful array of projects of late (Dune: Part Three, Primetime, The Odyssey, The Drama, et al), he’s the perfect person to breathe new life into this dated cinematic universe.

Last year, Bond fans received seismic news about the future of the classic British spy franchise: Barbara Broccoli and Michael G Wilson—the children of Albert R “Cubby” Broccoli, the legendary producer of the original James Bond films—who have overseen the series since the ’90s, ceded creative control to Amazon Studios. (The streamer bought MGM, the studio which produces the Bond movies, back in 2022, but has now reportedly shelled out an additional $1 billion for the right to freely develop this particular cinematic universe on its own terms.)

Then, a few months later, it emerged that Denis Villeneuve, the Oscar-nominated helmer of the Dune franchise, would be in the director’s chair. After that, we got some more intel, thanks to a report in Deadline. Villeneuve was said to have embarked on his search after completing production on Dune: Part Three.” At the time, fans learned he was apparently after “a fresh face”, ideally “an unknown,” possibly “in his late 20s or early 30s.”

According to reports in Deadline, The Hollywood Reporter and Variety, as well as Pattinson, the final screen tests featured Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, Josh O’Connor, and Nicholas Hoult. Harry Styles allegedly “had discussions about the part,” and Theo James was in the mix at one point, too.

As Pattinson emerges victorious, revisit his major competition below.

At 36, the Emmy-winning, Southampton-born actor was about the right age, and a beloved star of stage and screen—from Challengers and Disclosure Day to indie hits including God’s Own Country, La Chimera, and The Mastermind, and his latest stint on stage at the Almeida in Golden Boy. Given his impressive acting chops and penchant for working with internationally lauded auteurs, he would have been a high-brow choice in the Daniel Craig mould (lest we forget the latter was a critics’ favorite before he slipped on the tux).

Callum Turner as the next Bond? In many ways it made perfect sense. Dua Lipa’s husband is easy on the eyes and incredibly talented—just see his performances in Emma, Masters of the Air, and The Capture, the latter of which earned the Londoner a BAFTA TV nomination. Though not an unknown, he just about qualified as a fresh face.

His turn as a floppy-haired young intelligence officer in Apple TV+’s Slow Horses saw the gruff Scotsman enter the race, though when asked by British Vogue if he’d consider swapping River Cartwright’s low-key sweatshirts for a tuxedo and martini, he was coy. Of Daniel Craig, whom he grew up watching as 007, the actor said: “It’s going to take a long time to get him out of my head. Or anybody else’s.”

Brilliant in Nosferatu, The Great, and The Favourite, as well as more contemporary projects like The Menu, the 36-year-old from Berkshire had the action credentials (he’s Lex Luthor, after all) and the charisma for the top job.

Since his stint on The White Lotus, the dashing 41-year-old Oxford native had been considered a potential 007 (and The Gentlemen no doubt helped, too).

If you thought the musical superstar’s acting days were already behind him, post Dunkirk, Don’t Worry Darling, and My Policeman, then think again—apparently the opportunity to shrug on 007’s suit might have persuaded him back onto set.

#6720. What Is a Golden Retriever Boyfriend? Everything to Know About the Internet's Favorite Partner

Loyal, affectionate, and obsessed with you. What's not to love? Well, a few things, actually.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

There are two kinds of people in the dating world: those who think waiting three hours to text back makes them mysterious, and those who would happily text you three times in a row just because they saw a dog that reminded them of you. The latter? Probably a golden retriever boyfriend.

If you've spent any time on Dating TikTok, you've likely encountered the golden retriever boyfriend (or GRBF), a term for the kind of guy who's affectionate, enthusiastic, and almost aggressively happy to be in a relationship with you. Think Travis Kelce with Taylor Swift. Subtlety isn't exactly the selling point.

“A golden retriever boyfriend is the human equivalent of a tail wagging, wide-eyed pup who lives to love you,” explains Tiff Baira, relationship expert, matchmaker, “Streethearts” host, and author of Modern Dating for Dummies. “He’s sweet, loyal, emotionally available, and basically imprinted on you after the third date.”

Which, after one too many situationships with men who consider responding to a text an act of vulnerability, sounds pretty damn appealing. But is having a boyfriend who worships the ground you walk on always as great as TikTok makes it look? Not necessarily. Here's what experts want you to know about golden retriever boyfriends, including how to spot one and why all that devotion isn't automatically a recipe for relationship bliss.

“A golden retriever boyfriend is loving, positive, energetic, loyal, and uncomplicated,” says matchmaker and dating coach Blaine Anderson, founder of Dating By Blaine.

“He remembers your coffee order, sends good morning texts unprompted, and genuinely enjoys helping you build IKEA furniture,” adds Baira. “He’s not playing games; he’s the overly nice and caring type who puts you before himself.”

Sounds like the dream, right? Mostly. While the golden retriever boyfriend is known for being easygoing and devoted, that same enthusiasm can sometimes mean he's a little too eager to please.

“There may also be an undertone of ‘nice guy,’—specifically as opposed to ‘bad boy’—perhaps implying the golden retriever boyfriend is the guy you settle with after having your heart broken by a bad boy,” Anderson adds. Still, the term is generally a compliment. “You date the golden retriever guy because you don’t want to waste time on moody losers,” says Anderson.

As for how the trend got started? “The term ‘golden retriever boyfriend’ definitely gained traction on TikTok, where women proudly showed off their partners as the ultimate romantic prize,” Baira explains. “These were the guys who happily did whatever their girlfriend said.”

And in an era of dating app burnout and doomscrolling, “golden retriever boyfriends became a symbol of hope in a dating landscape flooded with horror stories,” says Baira. “Amid all that chaos, the GRBF became a mythical figure—an aspirational fantasy. Someone who might actually treat you like the princess you are.”

“If you’re wondering whether you’re with a golden retriever boyfriend, the signs are usually wagging right in front of you,” says Baira. “He lights up when he sees you, offers to carry your bag without being asked, and says, ‘let me know when you get home’ like it’s a sacred ritual.” In short, “There are no games, no ego—just someone who wants to be near you, build with you, and see you shine.”

On the flip side: “Does he make you worry he’s cheating? Have you come to expect he won’t buy you dinner? Does he only text at 10pm?” asks Anderson. In that case, “you do not have a golden retriever boyfriend!”

Of course, not every guy who seems like a golden retriever boyfriend actually is one. Enter “the wolf in golden retriever clothing—the guy who seems endlessly kind and selfless but secretly has an agenda,” says Baira. “If his niceness comes with strings attached, like using your connections, expecting money, or subtly manipulating you, he’s not golden; he’s gold plated.”

In other words, “Sometimes the nice guy isn’t actually nice at all,” Baira adds. “If he makes you feel like you owe him for his kindness or if his affection feels performative or transactional, that’s a clear sign it’s not real golden retriever energy.”

Some other red flags signaling you may be with a fake golden retriever? “He’s only sweet in public, performs affection when people are watching, and suddenly goes cold the second you’re alone,” says Baira. “He expects praise for basic things like texting back or showing up on time. He might flood you with compliments but makes subtle jabs when you're shining too bright.”

As much as TikTok might make you think golden retriever boyfriends are the end-all, they're not necessarily the right match for everyone.

“While every relationship is nuanced and there’s no universal good or bad, it’s important to remember that golden retriever energy still needs to match your energy,” says Baira. “Just because someone is nice to you doesn’t mean they’re right for you.”

Case in point: The Sex and the City love triangle between Carrie, Big, and Aidan. “On paper, Aidan was everything: warm, loving, emotionally present, always chasing Carrie,” says Baira. “But that’s just it. She didn’t want to be chased; she wanted to be challenged. And no matter how hard she tried to make him the one, he wasn’t.”

Dating coach Grace Lee, founder of A Good First Date, adds that for some people, golden retriever energy can feel stifling. “Doting affection can breed contempt. At first, it feels amazing to be adored so completely. But over time, you might feel overwhelmed—or even guilty,” she notes. “He’s so focused on making you happy that his own needs seem to disappear.” This can create an unhealthy dynamic where one partner constantly needs space while the other feels underappreciated.

That doesn't mean golden retriever boyfriends are inherently bad partners. It just means being nice isn't the same thing as being compatible, and staying in a relationship because it feels safe isn't necessarily the same as being happy in it.

Lee also warns that the Very Online nature of the golden retriever boyfriend trend can create unrealistic expectations, encouraging people to play a specific role in their relationships rather than address their actual needs.

“The hashtag acts as a checklist—for both the boyfriend and the girlfriend,” says Lee. “If you’re a GRBF, that means you’re a ‘good boyfriend.’ And if you’re a good boyfriend, then you don’t have to worry about the relationship falling apart. You’ve met the criteria. You’re safe from rejection. On the flip side, having a GRBF is proof that you’re desirable. You must be the kind of person who deserves that level of devotion. Instead of confronting your insecurities, you point to the list.”

Ultimately, a golden retriever boyfriend can absolutely be a great partner. But a relationship doesn't automatically work just because it checks all the right boxes on TikTok. What matters is whether you're actually happy together, not whether your boyfriend fits the internet's favorite dating archetype.

#6717. Poulet Basquaise

Rich tomatoes, stewed onions and peppers, and tender bone-in chicken make this Spanish- and French-inspired poulet Basquaise recipe extremely flavorful while still being surprisingly easy to make. Savory and rustic, it’s a stew that’s ideal for making ahead and enjoying all week long. The key ingredient? Piment d’Espelette—the regional spice known for its peppery, smoky essence. If you’re looking to master a classic dish or simply want a reliable, cozy weeknight dinner, then this recipe won’t let you down.

Heat a large skillet with oil and season the chicken with salt. Working in batches, add the chicken pieces and sear until the skin is golden in color, which should take 5 to 7 minutes. Remove the chicken to a heatproof plate.

Then reduce the heat and add the onion, bell peppers, garlic, piment d’Espelette, and salt. You’ll want to stir this once in a while so it doesn’t stick, but also let the vegetables soften. This should take 8 to 10 minutes.

At this point, increase the heat again and add the wine. Scrape up any bits on the bottom and allow those peppers and onions to absorb the wine. The liquid should reduce.

Add the tomatoes and broth and bring to a simmer. Be careful of splattering sauce and adjust the heat as needed. Place the chicken back into the skillet, skin side up, and cook until the chicken is tender and nearly falling off the bone. The sauce should also be thicker and fragrant. This should take about 30 to 40 minutes.

Stir in the chorizo and cook until it’s warmed through. Top it off with thyme and serve!

The full list of ingredients and instructions can be found in the recipe below.

Let cool and store in an airtight container in the refrigerator for up to 5 days. It’s certainly a great meal-prep dish. Make this on a Sunday night for lunch Monday and Tuesday.
